# Readings of twenty entries from mssEC 19 (War Department ciphers sent, March-December 1864)

Written 19 Sept 2026. The block between the markers is generated by `python3 decode.py --write` from
`ciphertext.txt` and the tables in `key.md`; `python3 decode.py --check` exits 1 if it is stale. Grades per
CLAUDE.md rule 4 apply to the code-word tokens: H = the meaning is written in the cipher book (mssEC 41, or for
four words the sister copy mssEC 43); C = only the Official Records print of this very telegram gives it; I =
fixed by another entry; M = uncertain. Plain English words are not graded; they are what the clerk wrote,
including the phonetic disguises ("Tap = pay han = nick", "tooth", "grotto" = go to, "opr rate", "a gain") and
the clerk's slips ("Prss", "mangled", "For rest").

Sixteen of the twenty were matched to an Official Records print by the subagent sweep of 26 volume-parts of
series I on the Internet Archive (or_check in NOTES.md section 4); the four it did not match (E4, E5, E6, E12)
were audited on 20 Sept 2026 (AUDIT.md): E6 is in OR I/32 pt 3 p.498 (the sweep missed it), E12 was printed
in 1864 and in every edition of Lincoln's works, E4 and E5 were not found in the sources searched (N3) and
may not be called unpublished. Every entry reads cleanly against the book; the seventeen OR-printed ones agree with the printed text word for word apart from
the clerk's slips and the times, which the OR rounds or omits (E2 7.40 vs 7.20; E14 10 vs 10.15; E16 12.30 vs
noon; E19 2 PM vs 1.40). The addressees at the Baltimore, Harpers Ferry and Cairo posts are the words the book
does not give (Prss/Praise, Submit, Mackerel/Mutton, Macbeth/Maynard): key.md section 7.

## Notes on particular readings

- The entries begin "Growl"/"Grapes" (= Washington, the place of origin), then the month in clear and the day in
  numeral words, then the time word; the shorter entries drop the place and the month. The operator's header
  gives the same date and often the same hour in clear, which fixes the time words independently.
- The signature words: Brutus/Infant = Secretary of War (Stanton, E1-E2); Ireland/Italy/Jacob/Jordan/Jew/Ivory/
  Inland = General-in-Chief (the book's meaning; in 1864 the signer is Halleck as Chief of Staff, and mssEC 43
  writes "Maj Gen H. W. Halleck" on the same lines); Ingress = President (Lincoln, E11, E12); Bender = Quarter-
  master General (Meigs, E5, E7); Jupiter/John = Grant.
- E4: "Baden Smyrna/Sidney" is read "Roanoke Inland" from the book; the meaning is written "Inland" on p.21 l.17
  but the sense (and Butler's reply) is Roanoke Island. "Camels" and "youths" are plain words that happen to be
  printed code words (Camel = Harpers Ferry, Youth = signature); they are marked plain in ciphertext.txt.
- E9: "for Mackerel tulip Energetic ..." - "tulip" is a book word (Open) but stands where the Grant-headquarters
  cipher of the Beckwith entries uses it as a punctuation word; the operator on the Harpers Ferry line seems to
  have slipped into that other cipher's habit. Left as the book reads it.
- E17: "pickted" (defeated: the OR prints "defeated"; the book's Putnam/Picket = Defeat, and the clerk wrote
  "pickted" for "picketed"), "Nansy" (south) and the doubled tail with a second signature: the whole tail after
  "Ireland" is the operator's service message to Clowry.
- E18: "Mammy duke" = Marmaduke, split by the clerk, "duke" marked plain (Duke = Meridian in the book).
- E11, E12 (Lincoln): the OR's "Yours of to-day" is the ledger's "Yours of Hedge"; Hedge is not in mssEC 41
  (Wedge/Wharf = Today), so it is graded C.

## Reconciliation of the transcription

Two subagents transcribed the ten pages independently (passes A and B, 19 Sept 2026; pass A of the first five
pages had to be rerun after an API refusal), without access to the volunteer text; the orchestrator compared
them word by word (difflib), took the volunteer transcription as third witness, and re-read the page image
(enlarged crops) at every disagreement not settled two to one. Decisions:

- E1 line 1: A "Prss[?]", B "Press", volunteers "Press"; the page reads "Prss" (the clerk's slip). Kept "Prss".
- E2: A "Country" / "men attacked", B "Counties" / "are attached" (volunteers "Counties", "were attached"); the
  image reads "Counties ... are attached". Kept B.
- E3 last words: A "Sure[?]", B "Lim[?]", volunteers "same here"; image "same here". Kept "same here".
- E4 line 2: A "Brandon[?]", B "Burden", volunteers "Baden"; the image reads "Baden" (a book word, Roanoke).
  Last line: A "Brenton[?]" (also the volunteers), B "Burden"; kept "Brenton[?]". (Superseded 24 Sept 2026: mssEC 25 p.77 reads "Buxton", applied to ciphertext.txt, H; NOTES.md.)
- E5: A "Pension[?]" / "Quarterly[?]" / "Amber[?]", B "Pensun[?]" / "Guernsey" / "Pender", volunteers "Pension" /
  "queenly" / "Bender"; image: "Pension", "queenly", "Bender". Kept the volunteers' readings ("queenly[?]").
- E7: A "Waymon[?]" / "Bender", B "Wayworn[?]" / "Pender", volunteers "wayworn" / "Bender"; image "wayworn",
  "Bender". Kept those.
- E9: A "Palseys[?]", B and volunteers "Palsys". Kept "Palsys". Both passes read "mangled" (image agrees).
- E10 line 1: A "Potato", B "Patate[?]", volunteers "Palate"; the clerk's t and l are alike; kept "Palate[?]" (the
  book word, Brigadier General; the OR prints "Brigadier-General Brayman"). Line 2: A "came Wrangler", B
  "bume[?] Wrangles", volunteers "burne wrangles"; image "Wash burne wrangles". Kept that. "For not" (both
  passes) is the clerk's "For rest" (Forrest): kept "For rest[?]".
- E11: A "received" / "conceive", B "recieved" / "concieve"; image has the -ei- spellings. Kept A (the
  reading is the same word).
- E12: A "Wol ford" / "Walnus" / "Warm", B "Wal ford" / "Walrus" / "Wyam[?]", volunteers "Wal ford" / "Walrus" /
  "warm". Kept B for the first two, "Warm[?]".
- E13: A "those", B "Thorn", volunteers "those"; kept "those[?]". B saw a loop after "Mutton"; it is a flourish.
  Both passes have "Frank" struck through at the head of the entry.
- E14: A "You request", B and volunteers "Your request". Kept "Your". Last word A "now[?]", B "Fran[?]",
  volunteers "van"; the image is a smudged "now". Kept "now[?]".
- E15: A "& Co", B "& Go"; image "& Co". A struck "tooth", B "tooth[?]": the stroke is a t-crossbar. Kept
  "tooth[?]". "walnus" (A) is "walrus" (B, volunteers).
- E16: A has a comma after "you" that B omits; kept.
- E17: A "Hanow[?]" / "Wavy[?]" / "Hurry", B "Hanover" / "Lowry" / "Henry", volunteers "Harrow" / "Nansy" /
  "Hurry"; image "Harrow" (the numeral 20, giving the 28th), "Nansy", "Hurry". Kept the volunteers' readings.
- E18: A "Harsh", B "Larch" (numeral 20: "harsh Paddle" = 28); A "Shannons", B "Shannon"; image "Harsh",
  "Shannons". Kept A. "Mammy" both; kept "Mammy[?]".
- E19: A "harsh", B "Larch"; image "harsh". A "so[?] it goes", B "[illeg] it you"; image "& so it goes" with a
  faint erased word before "it". Kept "so[?] it goes". B's interlined "authority" is on the page; A had it in
  the line. Kept as <ins>.
- E20: A "whist", B "whiskey"; image "whist" (a book word, Troops). Kept "whist".

Passes A and B agreed on every other word of the twenty entries; the volunteer transcription differs from the
reconciled text only in punctuation, capitalisation and the handful of words above.

Smith's division; he is also directed to send you all available troops in Saint Louis and vicinity; General Grant has sent his chief of staff, General Rawlins, to Saint Louis to hurry forward these reinforcements; should you want them to land at Clifton, Savannah or Nashville instead of Eastport please so direct; the four Michigan regiments had all gotten off by the 24th ultimo and the two from Ohio left the 24th and 27th; there is another regiment in Ohio which will be gotten off as soon as completed; the two regiments from the Northwest are en route, but as they had a long march before reaching railroad or steam-boat transportation, we may not hear of them for some days; I think you should concentrate all you can against Hood, replacing the garrisons in your rear by new troops as they arrive; printed OR I/41 pt 4 p.389 (page image, FV-MS18r) (MS18-R9; row 9882/0; leaf image-read at 2400 px, whole entry)**

**E601 | Page 166 | 9826 | mssEC 18 (obj 10074, pointer 9826; printed page 160), 25 Aug 1864 Washington (10.30 PM by the decoder: print 9 p.m., not visible on the leaf header, M), R. R. McCaine (operator), for Sheridan (C by print), signed C. C. Augur, Maj. Gen. Commanding Dept. of Washington ('Nicarangua', C by print): among the persons brought in by Major Wait is one well known to me as a reliable Union man who has heretofore given me valuable information; he is from Upperville and says he heard no talk there of the rebel army intending to move this way; says they are conscripting everybody there capable of bearing arms, those who join Mosby being exempt from conscription [into] Lee's army; by this means Mosby can command between 800 and 1000 men; to get information from Snicker's Gap would require a force able to manage Mosby, whose headquarters are on the route there; small parties will be picked up; I will send the 8th Illinois Cavalry again to that vicinity as soon as it can move, and will send with it one of the regiments' [..] from Falls Church; to clean out Loudoun County and destroy the crops there will require a much larger force than I can send; I will do all I can, however; the horses of the 8th Illinois have to be shod before they can move; I will let you know tomorrow when they will move; I have a man at Middleburg who is employed to give me the earliest information of any movement of the rebels in this direction; I trust he will not deceive me; in print, OR I/43 pt 1 p.909 (and Williamson, Mosby's Rangers, 1896; FV-L14a) (L14-A; row 9826/0)**
